I don’t sit down and feel scared of the food or uncomfortable because of what eating means. It’s more like eating just hasn’t been happening normally, and then when it does, it feels like this awkward chore.\n\nLike, the thing that made me bring it up is that over the past few weeks I’ll get through the morning and realize I never had breakfast, and then somehow lunch doesn’t happen either. It’s not really intentional—I’m not thinking, “I shouldn’t eat.” It just kind of slips by. Then at dinner my wife will have cooked something, and I’ll sit there trying to eat because I know I should, and because she went to the trouble, but I mostly just pick at it.\n\nAnd then I noticed my jeans were feeling loose, which kind of made it harder to ignore. So it’s not anxiety about eating as much as my appetite being off and meals feeling harder than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel like eating if someone else prepared the meal and you didn't have to make an effort?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but honestly even when someone else does prepare it, it’s still been kind of hard. Like with dinner, my wife will cook, so the effort part is already taken care of, and I’ll still just sort of sit there and pick at it.\n\nThat’s kind of what made me notice it wasn’t just me being lazy about cooking or forgetting to make something. For the past few weeks, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really deciding to, and then by dinner there’s food right in front of me and I know I should eat it, but I don’t have much appetite. I’ll take a few bites, move things around on the plate, that kind of thing.\n\nAnd then my jeans started feeling loose, which made me think, okay, maybe this is more of a real change than I was admitting. So I guess having someone else make the meal helps with the effort part, but it doesn’t really bring my appetite back the way I’d expect.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite consistent throughout the day, or are there specific times when you feel less like eating?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly noticeable earlier in the day, I think. Like breakfast just… doesn’t happen. I’ll wake up and I don’t feel hungry, and then I’ll sort of drift into the day, and before I know it it’s late morning or afternoon and I realize I never ate anything.\n\nLunch is kind of the same way. It’s not like I’m sitting there fighting the urge to eat or anything. It just doesn’t occur to me the way it normally would, or if it does, it feels easy to put off.\n\nDinner is the time when I’m more aware of it, mostly because my wife has usually cooked and there’s actual food in front of me. But even then I’m not really hungry. I’ll try to eat, but I end up picking at it more than having a real meal.\n\nSo I guess it’s not totally different at different times—it’s been off all day—but breakfast and lunch are the ones I skip without meaning to, and dinner is where I notice I’m forcing it a little. My jeans feeling loose is what made me think, okay, this has probably been going on enough that it’s not just a random day or two.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of appetite is related to your feelings of being down or sad?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is related. I mean, I don’t know which one is causing which, exactly, but they seem to come together.\n\nMost days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling right away. Not like I’m crying or anything dramatic necessarily, just like everything is muted. The apartment feels too quiet, and I’ll kind of sit there knowing I should start the day, but it feels like there’s no pull toward anything.\n\nEven picking up my guitar doesn’t really lift me the way it usually would. I’ll hold it or strum a little, and it’s like… nothing really catches. So when I’m feeling like that, food just doesn’t seem appealing either. It’s not that I’m upset about eating, it’s more like the same flatness applies to meals too. Like breakfast doesn’t seem worth making, lunch doesn’t occur to me, and dinner is just something I’m supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have your eating habits been like this for as long as you can remember, or is this a relatively new change?\n\nPatient: No, it’s definitely not how I’ve always been. I mean, I’ve never been the most structured person with meals—like I can forget lunch if I’m distracted or whatever—but this feels different.\n\nThis has really been more of a past few weeks kind of thing. I’ll wake up and just not eat breakfast, not because I made some decision to skip it, just because it doesn’t even feel like something I want. Then lunch kind of disappears the same way, and by the time dinner comes around my wife will have cooked, so it’s not like there’s an excuse about effort, and I still mostly just pick at it.\n\nI think what made me actually bring it up is my jeans started feeling loose. That was the point where I thought, okay, this isn’t just a random couple of missed meals. It’s been enough of a change that I can notice it physically. So yeah, relatively new.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite changes when you're feeling particularly down or sad?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does seem to shift with that. When I’m feeling more down, food is one of the first things that kind of drops off without me making a conscious decision about it.\n\nLike, over the past few weeks, my eating has just been really off. I’ll skip breakfast without meaning to, and then lunch sort of disappears too. And then by dinner, even if my wife has cooked and the food is right there, I’ll just sort of pick at it instead of actually eating a normal meal.\n\nI think I noticed it more because my jeans have started feeling loose, and that made it feel less like just a bad day here and there and more like, okay, this has been going on for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating would go back to normal if you were feeling happier and less down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t prove it, obviously, but it feels connected. I’ll just skip breakfast without really deciding to, then lunch kind of passes by too, and by dinner my wife will have cooked something and I’ll still just pick at it like I’m not really interested. And then my jeans started feeling loose, which made me think, okay, this is probably not just me being distracted or lazy about meals.\n\nSo yeah… if I were feeling happier, or even just less flat, I’d expect my appetite would probably come back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appetite would improve if your mood were to improve?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do think it probably would. I mean, it feels pretty tied together, because this isn’t how I normally am with food.\n\nLike I said, what made me even bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been off in this weird, passive way. I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to — it’s not like I’m trying to diet or making some plan — it just doesn’t happen. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked and it’s right there in front of me, I’ll mostly just pick at it.\n\nAnd the thing that made me go, okay, maybe this is actually something, is my jeans are starting to feel loose. So yeah, if my mood lifted, I’d expect my appetite would probably start coming back too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would return to normal if your sadness and heavy feelings were to lift?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it would. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but it really does seem like the eating piece follows how heavy I’m feeling.\n\nWhen I’m more like myself, I don’t usually have to think so hard about meals. But these past few weeks, it’s been… I don’t know, almost like food just falls off the list. I’ll skip breakfast without even deciding to, and then suddenly it’s afternoon and I haven’t eaten lunch either. Then at dinner, even if my wife cooked and it’s right there, I’ll just sort of move it around or take a few bites and stop.\n\nAnd I think the reason it stood out to me is my jeans have started feeling loose. That made it feel less like just being distracted and more like, okay, my appetite really has changed. So yeah, if the sadness and that heavy feeling lifted, I’d expect my eating would probably go back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you were in a different environment or with different people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s mainly about the environment or who I’m with, honestly. I mean, maybe if someone was really actively putting food in front of me or reminding me, I might eat a little more, but the thing I’m noticing is happening even at home, in my usual routine, with my wife there.\n\nLike, what made me bring it up is that over the past few weeks my eating has just been off. I’ll skip breakfast without really meaning to, then lunch kind of disappears too, and it’s not like I’m making a choice or trying to avoid food. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked something — which normally I’d appreciate and eat — I just pick at it. I’ll take a few bites and kind of lose interest.\n\nAnd then my jeans starting to feel loose made me think, okay, this is more than just being in the wrong place or around the wrong people. So… no, I don’t think a different environment would fully fix it. It feels more like my appetite itself has changed lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would improve if your sadness and heavy feelings were to completely go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t prove it, obviously, but it really feels connected. When that heavy feeling is there, eating just sort of… stops being something I naturally do.\n\nWhat made me bring it up in the first place is that the past few weeks have been noticeably different for me. It’s more like I just don’t get around to it, and then suddenly it’s lunchtime, and that slips by too. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked and the food is right there, I’ll sit there and kind of pick at it, take a few bites, move it around, and then I’m done.\n\nAnd that’s not really normal for me. The thing that made it feel more real was my jeans starting to feel loose. So I guess having someone else make the meal helps with the effort part, but it doesn’t really bring my appetite back the way I’d expect.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite consistent throughout the day, or are there specific times when you feel less like eating?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly noticeable earlier in the day, I think. Like breakfast just… doesn’t happen. I’ll wake up and I don’t feel hungry, and then I’ll sort of drift into the day, and before I know it it’s late morning or afternoon and I realize I never ate anything.\n\nLunch is kind of the same way. It’s not like I’m sitting there fighting the urge to eat or anything. It just doesn’t occur to me the way it normally would, or if it does, it feels easy to put off.\n\nDinner is the time when I’m more aware of it, mostly because my wife has usually cooked and there’s actual food in front of me. But even then I’m not really hungry. I’ll try to eat, but I end up picking at it more than having a real meal.\n\nSo I guess it’s not totally different at different times—it’s been off all day—but breakfast and lunch are the ones I skip without meaning to, and dinner is where I notice I’m forcing it a little. My jeans feeling loose is what made me think, okay, this has probably been going on enough that it’s not just a random day or two.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of appetite is related to your feelings of being down or sad?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is related. I mean, I don’t know which one is causing which, exactly, but they seem to come together.\n\nMost days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling right away. Not like I’m crying or anything dramatic necessarily, just like everything is muted. The apartment feels too quiet, and I’ll kind of sit there knowing I should start the day, but it feels like there’s no pull toward anything.\n\nEven picking up my guitar doesn’t really lift me the way it usually would. I’ll hold it or strum a little, and it’s like… nothing really catches. So when I’m feeling like that, food just doesn’t seem appealing either. It’s not that I’m upset about eating, it’s more like the same flatness applies to meals too. Like breakfast doesn’t seem worth making, lunch doesn’t occur to me, and dinner is just something I’m supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have your eating habits been like this for as long as you can remember, or is this a relatively new change?\n\nPatient: No, it’s definitely not how I’ve always been. I mean, I’ve never been the most structured person with meals—like I can forget lunch if I’m distracted or whatever—but this feels different.\n\nThis has really been more of a past few weeks kind of thing. I’ll wake up and just not eat breakfast, not because I made some decision to skip it, just because it doesn’t even feel like something I want. Then lunch kind of disappears the same way, and by the time dinner comes around my wife will have cooked, so it’s not like there’s an excuse about effort, and I still mostly just pick at it.\n\nI think what made me actually bring it up is my jeans started feeling loose. That was the point where I thought, okay, this isn’t just a random couple of missed meals. It’s been enough of a change that I can notice it physically. So yeah, relatively new.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite changes when you're feeling particularly down or sad?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does seem to shift with that. When I’m feeling more down, food is one of the first things that kind of drops off without me making a conscious decision about it.\n\nLike, over the past few weeks, my eating has just been really off. I’ll skip breakfast without meaning to, and then lunch sort of disappears too. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I don’t want to eat,” exactly—it’s more like I don’t have the appetite or the momentum to make it happen. And then by dinner, even if my wife has cooked and the food is right there, I’ll just sort of pick at it instead of actually eating a normal meal.\n\nI think I noticed it more because my jeans have started feeling loose, and that made it feel less like just a bad day here and there and more like, okay, this has been going on for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating would go back to normal if you were feeling happier and less down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t prove it, obviously, but it feels connected. When I’m not as weighed down, eating is more normal for me — I don’t usually have to remind myself to do something as basic as breakfast or lunch.\n\nWhat made me bring it up is that the past few weeks have felt different. I’ll just skip breakfast without really deciding to, then lunch kind of passes by too, and by dinner my wife will have cooked something and I’ll still just pick at it like I’m not really interested. And then my jeans started feeling loose, which made me think, okay, this is probably not just me being distracted or lazy about meals.\n\nSo yeah… if I were feeling happier, or even just less flat, I’d expect my appetite would probably come back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appetite would improve if your mood were to improve?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do think it probably would. I mean, it feels pretty tied together, because this isn’t how I normally am with food.\n\nLike I said, what made me even bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been off in this weird, passive way. I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to — it’s not like I’m trying to diet or making some plan — it just doesn’t happen. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked and it’s right there in front of me, I’ll mostly just pick at it.\n\nAnd the thing that made me go, okay, maybe this is actually something, is my jeans are starting to feel loose. So yeah, if my mood lifted, I’d expect my appetite would probably start coming back too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would return to normal if your sadness and heavy feelings were to lift?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it would. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but it really does seem like the eating piece follows how heavy I’m feeling.\n\nWhen I’m more like myself, I don’t usually have to think so hard about meals. But these past few weeks, it’s been… I don’t know, almost like food just falls off the list. I’ll skip breakfast without even deciding to, and then suddenly it’s afternoon and I haven’t eaten lunch either. Then at dinner, even if my wife cooked and it’s right there, I’ll just sort of move it around or take a few bites and stop.\n\nAnd I think the reason it stood out to me is my jeans have started feeling loose. That made it feel less like just being distracted and more like, okay, my appetite really has changed. So yeah, if the sadness and that heavy feeling lifted, I’d expect my eating would probably go back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you were in a different environment or with different people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s mainly about the environment or who I’m with, honestly. I mean, maybe if someone was really actively putting food in front of me or reminding me, I might eat a little more, but the thing I’m noticing is happening even at home, in my usual routine, with my wife there.\n\nLike, what made me bring it up is that over the past few weeks my eating has just been off. I’ll skip breakfast without really meaning to, then lunch kind of disappears too, and it’s not like I’m making a choice or trying to avoid food. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked something — which normally I’d appreciate and eat — I just pick at it. I’ll take a few bites and kind of lose interest.\n\nAnd then my jeans starting to feel loose made me think, okay, this is more than just being in the wrong place or around the wrong people. So… no, I don’t think a different environment would fully fix it. It feels more like my appetite itself has changed lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would improve if your sadness and heavy feelings were to completely go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t prove it, obviously, but it really feels connected. When that heavy feeling is there, eating just sort of… stops being something I naturally do.\n\nWhat made me bring it up in the first place is that the past few weeks have been noticeably different for me. I’ll wake up and not eat breakfast, but not in a deliberate way — it’s not like I’m choosing to skip it or trying to lose weight. It’s more like I just don’t get around to it, and then suddenly it’s lunchtime, and that slips by too. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked and the food is right there, I’ll sit there and kind of pick at it, take a few bites, move it around, and then I’m done.\n\nAnd that’s not really normal for me. The thing that made it feel more real was my jeans starting to feel loose. So yeah, if the sadness and heaviness completely went away, I’d expect my eating would probably improve and get closer to how it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few weeks, such as more than 5% of your body weight?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s that much, like I haven’t actually weighed myself and done the math or anything. I’m not trying to diet, no. That part is clear — it’s not intentional, and I’m not avoiding food because of weight or anything like that.

It’s more that I noticed my jeans are starting to feel loose, and that seemed weird because I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nIt’s been over the past few weeks, I think. Like I said, I’ll just accidentally miss breakfast and lunch, and then by dinner I’m not really eating much either—just kind of moving food around or taking a few bites even when my wife cooked. So I assume I’ve lost some weight, but I couldn’t give you a number.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don’t think that’s what’s going on. I’m not avoiding food because I’m scared of gaining weight or anything like that. It’s more almost the opposite, like what made me notice it was that I realized my jeans were getting loose, and I hadn’t been trying to make that happen.\n\nIt’s just been this weird thing the last few weeks where breakfast doesn’t happen, then lunch doesn’t happen, and I don’t even really register it until later. Then dinner comes around and my wife has made something, and I’ll sit there feeling kind of guilty because I’m just picking at it. It’s not about the food or about wanting to be thinner. I just don’t feel much appetite, or eating feels like more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed some big, obvious change where I’m like, “I don’t care about anything anymore,” if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere are things I normally like doing, but I guess I haven’t really been paying close attention to whether I’m enjoying them less or just not getting around to them. Sometimes it all kind of blends together, you know? Like it’s hard for me to tell if I’m not interested, or if I’m just distracted, or tired, or whatever else. I wouldn’t say there’s one activity where I’m like, “Oh, I used to love that and now I hate it.” It’s more… I don’t know, I haven’t really noticed it clearly enough to say yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t always call it “sad,” exactly, because it’s not like I’m crying all the time or anything dramatic like that. It’s more like most days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling already there, before anything has even happened.\n\nThe apartment will feel really quiet, even if nothing is wrong, and I’ll just kind of sit with that for a while. It’s like the whole day starts out muted. I’ll try to do something that usually helps, like pick up my guitar, and even that doesn’t really lift me much. I can play a little, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually feeling better.\n\nSo, yeah, I’d say I’ve been feeling down most of the time lately, just in a kind of low, heavy way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uncomfortable when you think about eating or when you're actually eating?\n\nPatient: Not anxious, exactly. I don’t sit down and feel scared of the food or uncomfortable because of what eating means. It’s more like eating just hasn’t been happening normally, and then when it does, it feels like this awkward chore.\n\nLike, the thing that made me bring it up is that over the past few weeks I’ll get through the morning and realize I never had breakfast, and then somehow lunch doesn’t happen either. It’s not really intentional—I’m not thinking, “I shouldn’t eat.” It just kind of slips by. Then at dinner my wife will have cooked something, and I’ll sit there trying to eat because I know I should, and because she went to the trouble, but I mostly just pick at it.\n\nAnd then I noticed my jeans were feeling loose, which kind of made it harder to ignore. So it’s not anxiety about eating as much as my appetite being off and meals feeling harder than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel like eating if someone else prepared the meal and you didn't have to make an effort?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but honestly even when someone else does prepare it, it’s still been kind of hard. Like with dinner, my wife will cook, so the effort part is already taken care of, and I’ll still just sort of sit there and pick at it.\n\nThat’s kind of what made me notice it wasn’t just me being lazy about cooking or forgetting to make something. For the past few weeks, I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really deciding to, and then by dinner there’s food right in front of me and I know I should eat it, but I don’t have much appetite. I’ll take a few bites, move things around on the plate, that kind of thing.\n\nAnd then my jeans started feeling loose, which made me think, okay, maybe this is more of a real change than I was admitting. So I guess having someone else make the meal helps with the effort part, but it doesn’t really bring my appetite back the way I’d expect.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite consistent throughout the day, or are there specific times when you feel less like eating?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly noticeable earlier in the day, I think. Like breakfast just… doesn’t happen. I’ll wake up and I don’t feel hungry, and then I’ll sort of drift into the day, and before I know it it’s late morning or afternoon and I realize I never ate anything.\n\nLunch is kind of the same way. It’s not like I’m sitting there fighting the urge to eat or anything. It just doesn’t occur to me the way it normally would, or if it does, it feels easy to put off.\n\nDinner is the time when I’m more aware of it, mostly because my wife has usually cooked and there’s actual food in front of me. But even then I’m not really hungry. I’ll try to eat, but I end up picking at it more than having a real meal.\n\nSo I guess it’s not totally different at different times—it’s been off all day—but breakfast and lunch are the ones I skip without meaning to, and dinner is where I notice I’m forcing it a little. My jeans feeling loose is what made me think, okay, this has probably been going on enough that it’s not just a random day or two.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of appetite is related to your feelings of being down or sad?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is related. I mean, I don’t know which one is causing which, exactly, but they seem to come together.\n\nMost days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling right away. Not like I’m crying or anything dramatic necessarily, just like everything is muted. The apartment feels too quiet, and I’ll kind of sit there knowing I should start the day, but it feels like there’s no pull toward anything.\n\nEven picking up my guitar doesn’t really lift me the way it usually would. I’ll hold it or strum a little, and it’s like… nothing really catches. So when I’m feeling like that, food just doesn’t seem appealing either. It’s not that I’m upset about eating, it’s more like the same flatness applies to meals too. Like breakfast doesn’t seem worth making, lunch doesn’t occur to me, and dinner is just something I’m supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have your eating habits been like this for as long as you can remember, or is this a relatively new change?\n\nPatient: No, it’s definitely not how I’ve always been. I mean, I’ve never been the most structured person with meals—like I can forget lunch if I’m distracted or whatever—but this feels different.\n\nThis has really been more of a past few weeks kind of thing. I’ll wake up and just not eat breakfast, not because I made some decision to skip it, just because it doesn’t even feel like something I want. Then lunch kind of disappears the same way, and by the time dinner comes around my wife will have cooked, so it’s not like there’s an excuse about effort, and I still mostly just pick at it.\n\nI think what made me actually bring it up is my jeans started feeling loose. That was the point where I thought, okay, this isn’t just a random couple of missed meals. It’s been enough of a change that I can notice it physically. So yeah, relatively new.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite changes when you're feeling particularly down or sad?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does seem to shift with that. When I’m feeling more down, food is one of the first things that kind of drops off without me making a conscious decision about it.\n\nLike, over the past few weeks, my eating has just been really off. I’ll skip breakfast without meaning to, and then lunch sort of disappears too. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I don’t want to eat,” exactly—it’s more like I don’t have the appetite or the momentum to make it happen. And then by dinner, even if my wife has cooked and the food is right there, I’ll just sort of pick at it instead of actually eating a normal meal.\n\nI think I noticed it more because my jeans have started feeling loose, and that made it feel less like just a bad day here and there and more like, okay, this has been going on for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating would go back to normal if you were feeling happier and less down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t prove it, obviously, but it feels connected. When I’m not as weighed down, eating is more normal for me — I don’t usually have to remind myself to do something as basic as breakfast or lunch.\n\nWhat made me bring it up is that the past few weeks have felt different. I’ll just skip breakfast without really deciding to, then lunch kind of passes by too, and by dinner my wife will have cooked something and I’ll still just pick at it like I’m not really interested. And then my jeans started feeling loose, which made me think, okay, this is probably not just me being distracted or lazy about meals.\n\nSo yeah… if I were feeling happier, or even just less flat, I’d expect my appetite would probably come back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appetite would improve if your mood were to improve?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do think it probably would. I mean, it feels pretty tied together, because this isn’t how I normally am with food.\n\nLike I said, what made me even bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been off in this weird, passive way. I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to — it’s not like I’m trying to diet or making some plan — it just doesn’t happen. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked and it’s right there in front of me, I’ll mostly just pick at it.\n\nAnd the thing that made me go, okay, maybe this is actually something, is my jeans are starting to feel loose. So yeah, if my mood lifted, I’d expect my appetite would probably start coming back too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would return to normal if your sadness and heavy feelings were to lift?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it would. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but it really does seem like the eating piece follows how heavy I’m feeling.\n\nWhen I’m more like myself, I don’t usually have to think so hard about meals. But these past few weeks, it’s been… I don’t know, almost like food just falls off the list. I’ll skip breakfast without even deciding to, and then suddenly it’s afternoon and I haven’t eaten lunch either. Then at dinner, even if my wife cooked and it’s right there, I’ll just sort of move it around or take a few bites and stop.\n\nAnd I think the reason it stood out to me is my jeans have started feeling loose. That made it feel less like just being distracted and more like, okay, my appetite really has changed. So yeah, if the sadness and that heavy feeling lifted, I’d expect my eating would probably go back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you were in a different environment or with different people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s mainly about the environment or who I’m with, honestly. I mean, maybe if someone was really actively putting food in front of me or reminding me, I might eat a little more, but the thing I’m noticing is happening even at home, in my usual routine, with my wife there.\n\nLike, what made me bring it up is that over the past few weeks my eating has just been off. I’ll skip breakfast without really meaning to, then lunch kind of disappears too, and it’s not like I’m making a choice or trying to avoid food. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked something — which normally I’d appreciate and eat — I just pick at it. I’ll take a few bites and kind of lose interest.\n\nAnd then my jeans starting to feel loose made me think, okay, this is more than just being in the wrong place or around the wrong people. So… no, I don’t think a different environment would fully fix it. It feels more like my appetite itself has changed lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would improve if your sadness and heavy feelings were to completely go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t prove it, obviously, but it really feels connected. When that heavy feeling is there, eating just sort of… stops being something I naturally do.\n\nWhat made me bring it up in the first place is that the past few weeks have been noticeably different for me. I’ll wake up and not eat breakfast, but not in a deliberate way — it’s not like I’m choosing to skip it or trying to lose weight. It’s more like I just don’t get around to it, and then suddenly it’s lunchtime, and that slips by too. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked and the food is right there, I’ll sit there and kind of pick at it, take a few bites, move it around, and then I’m done.\n\nAnd that’s not really normal for me. The thing that made it feel more real was my jeans starting to feel loose. So yeah, if the sadness and heaviness completely went away, I’d expect my eating would probably improve and get closer to how it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few weeks, such as more than 5% of your body weight?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s that much, like I haven’t actually weighed myself and done the math or anything. So I don’t want to say yes to more than 5% if I don’t really know.\n\nWhat I have noticed is more… the practical stuff, I guess. For the past few weeks my eating has been off in a way that feels pretty noticeable to me. I’ll skip breakfast without really meaning to, and then lunch kind of gets skipped too, not because I’m trying to or anything, just because I don’t have much appetite or I don’t think about it. Then dinner comes around and even if my wife made food, I’ll mostly pick at it, take a few bites, and then I’m done.\n\nAnd my jeans have started feeling loose, which is what made me think I probably have lost some weight. But I don’t know the exact number or whether it’s more than 5%.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can honestly say it’s more than 5%, because I haven’t weighed myself. I know you asked that before, and I keep coming back to the same answer — I just don’t have a number.\n\nWhat I can say is it feels like I’ve lost some weight, or at least something has changed, because my jeans are starting to feel loose. And the reason I brought it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has been really off. I’ll skip breakfast without meaning to, then lunch sort of disappears too, and by dinner, even if my wife cooked something, I’ll just pick at it and only take a few bites.\n\nSo… more than 5%, I’m not sure. But there’s definitely been a noticeable appetite change and my clothes feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant weight loss, such as more than 5% of your body weight, over the past few weeks without trying to diet?\n\nPatient: I’m not trying to diet, no. That part is clear — it’s not intentional, and I’m not avoiding food because of weight or anything like that.\n\nAs far as whether it’s “significant” like more than 5%, I still don’t really know because I haven’t stepped on a scale. And then my jeans started feeling loose, which made me think, okay, this is probably not just me being distracted or lazy about meals.\n\nSo yeah… if I were feeling happier, or even just less flat, I’d expect my appetite would probably come back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appetite would improve if your mood were to improve?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do think it probably would. I mean, it feels pretty tied together, because this isn’t how I normally am with food.\n\nLike I said, what made me even bring it up is that for the past few weeks my eating has just been off in this weird, passive way. Then dinner comes around, and even when my wife has cooked and it’s right there in front of me, I’ll mostly just pick at it.\n\nAnd the thing that made me go, okay, maybe this is actually something, is my jeans are starting to feel loose. So yeah, if my mood lifted, I’d expect my appetite would probably start coming back too.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would return to normal if your sadness and heavy feelings were to lift?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it would. That made it feel less like just being distracted and more like, okay, my appetite really has changed. So yeah, if the sadness and that heavy feeling lifted, I’d expect my eating would probably go back closer to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you were in a different environment or with different people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s mainly about the environment or who I’m with, honestly. I mean, maybe if someone was really actively putting food in front of me or reminding me, I might eat a little more, but the thing I’m noticing is happening even at home, in my usual routine, with my wife there.\n\nLike, what made me bring it up is that over the past few weeks my eating has just been off. I’ll take a few bites and kind of lose interest.\n\nAnd then my jeans starting to feel loose made me think, okay, this is more than just being in the wrong place or around the wrong people. So… no, I don’t think a different environment would fully fix it. It feels more like my appetite itself has changed lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would improve if your sadness and heavy feelings were to completely go away?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked and the food is right there, I’ll sit there and kind of pick at it, take a few bites, move it around, and then I’m done.\n\nAnd that’s not really normal for me. So yeah, if the sadness and heaviness completely went away, I’d expect my eating would probably improve and get closer to how it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few weeks, such as more than 5% of your body weight?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if it’s that much, like I haven’t actually weighed myself and done the math or anything. So I don’t want to say yes to more than 5% if I don’t really know.\n\nWhat I have noticed is more… the practical stuff, I guess. Then dinner comes around and even if my wife made food, I’ll mostly pick at it, take a few bites, and then I’m done.\n\nAnd my jeans have started feeling loose, which is what made me think I probably have lost some weight.
